are given explicitly as follows

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\penalty\ \textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{unfold}}}}}}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=\begin{bmatrix}{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{:,:,1}\\
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{:,:,2}\\
\vdots\\
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{:,:,{\sf c}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{m{\sf c}\times n},\quad\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{unfold}}}}}}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})=\begin{bmatrix}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{:,1,1}\\
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{:,1,2}\\
\vdots\\
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{:,1,{\sf c}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n{\sf c}}.

Since Eq. (
17
) is a straightforward reformulation of the definition of t-product in
[
36
, Definition 6.3]
, the proof is simply omitted.

According to Lemma
16
, we have the following remark on the relationship between t-NNs and fully connected neural networks (FNNs).

Remark

(Connnection with FNNs)
.

The t-NNs and FNNs can be treated as special cases of each other.

(I)

When the channel number
{\sf c}=1
, the t-product becomes to standard matrix multi-lication and the proposed t-NN predictor Eq. (
5
) degenerates to an
(L+1)
-layer FNN, which means the FNN is a special case of the t-NN.

(II)

On the other hand, by the definition of t-product, the t-NN
f(\cdot;{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
in Eq. (
5
) has the compounding representation as an FNN:

\displaystyle{{{\displaystyle\penalty\ f_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}=\textnormal{{w}}\circ\sigma\circ\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{op}}}}}}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)})\circ\sigma\circ\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{op}}}}}}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L-1)})\circ\cdots\circ\sigma\circ\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{op}}}}}}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}).

Thus, t-NN can also be seen as a special case of FNN.

Appendix C
Standarad and Adversarial Generalization Bounds for t-NNs

C.1
Standarad Generalization Bound for t-NNs

Lemma 17
.

Consider the ReLU activation.
For any t-vector-valued function set
{\cal H}
and any convex and monotonically
increasing function
g:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\left[\sup_{\textnormal{{h}}\in{\cal H},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}:\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq R}g\left(\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\textnormal{{h}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\right)\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\left[\sup_{\textnormal{{h}}\in{\cal H}}g\left(R\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\textnormal{{h}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\right)\right],

where
R>0
is a constant.

Proof.

This lemma is a direct corollary of Lemma 1 in
Ref.
[
8
]
by using Eq. (
17
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
3
.

According to Lemma
29
, we can upper bound the generalization error of
\ell\circ f
for any
f\in\mathfrak{F}
through the (empirical) Rademacher complexity
\hat{R}_{S}(\ell\circ\mathfrak{F})
where
\ell\circ\mathfrak{F}:=\{\ell\circ f\penalty\ |\penalty\ f\in\mathfrak{F}\}
. Further regarding the
L_{\ell}
-Lipschitzness
7
7

7

This is a natural consequence of
(A.2)
in Assumption
2
. See Eq. (
7
).
of the loss function
\ell
, we have

\hat{R}_{S}(\ell\circ\mathfrak{F})\leq L_{\ell}\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F})

by the Talagrand’s contraction lemma (Lemma
30
). Then, it remains to bound
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F})
.

To upper bound
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F})
,
we follow the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 1]
. By Jensen’s inequality, the (scaled) Rademacher complexity
N\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F})=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{f\in\mathfrak{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})
satisfies

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{\lambda}\log\exp\left(\lambda\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{f\in\mathfrak{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})\right)\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{f\in\mathfrak{F}}\exp\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})\right),

(18)

where
\lambda>0
is an arbitrary parameter. Then, we can use a “peeling” argument
[
37
,
8
]
as follows.

The Rademacher complexity can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\penalty\ N\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{\textnormal{{f}}^{(L)},\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}}\right\|_{2}\leq B_{w}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\textnormal{{f}}^{(L)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})

(19)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}}\right\|_{2}\leq B_{w}\\
\textnormal{{f}}^{(L-1)},\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{L}\end{subarray}}\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\sigma\bigg({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}*_{M}\textnormal{{f}}^{(L-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{{f}}^{(L-1)}\\
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{L}\end{subarray}}\exp\left(B_{w}\lambda\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\sigma\bigg({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}*_{M}\textnormal{{f}}^{(L-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})\bigg)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(2\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textnormal{{f}}^{(L-2)}\\
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L-1)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{L-1}\end{subarray}}\exp\left(B_{w}B_{L}\lambda\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\bigg({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L-1)}*_{M}\textnormal{{f}}^{(L-2)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})\bigg)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\cdots

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(2^{L-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{1}\end{subarray}}\exp\left(B_{w}\prod_{l=2}^{L}B_{l}\lambda\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\bigg({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\textnormal{{f}}^{(0)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i})\bigg)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(2^{L}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\exp\left(B_{w}\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}\lambda\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\right)\right).

Letting
B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}=B_{w}\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}
, define a random variable

\displaystyle\penalty\ Z=B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\cdot\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

as a function of random variables
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i}
.
Then

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(2^{L}\cdot\mathbb{E}\exp\lambda Z\right)=\frac{L\log 2}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\big(\mathbb{E}\exp\lambda(Z-\mathbb{E}Z)\big)+\mathbb{E}Z.

(20)

By Jensen’s inequlity,
\mathbb{E}Z
can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{E}Z

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle=B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\mathbb{E}\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\|{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}=B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\left[\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}\varepsilon_{j}(\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}))^{\top}(\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{j}))\right]}

\displaystyle=B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}.

To handle the
\log\big(\mathbb{E}\exp\lambda(Z-\mathbb{E}Z)\big)
term in Eq. (
20
) , note that
Z
is a deterministic function of the
i.i.d.
random variables
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i}
, and satisfies

\displaystyle\penalty\ Z(\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,\varepsilon_{i},\cdots,\varepsilon_{m})-Z(\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,-\varepsilon_{i},\cdots,\varepsilon_{m})\leq 2B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}.

This means that
Z
satisfies a bounded-difference condition , which by the proof of
[
5
, Theorem 6.2]
, implies that
Z
is sub-Gaussian, with the variance factor

\displaystyle\penalty\ v=\frac{1}{4}(2B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}})^{2}=B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}.

and satisfies

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{\lambda}\log\big(\mathbb{E}\exp\lambda(Z-\mathbb{E}Z)\big)\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\frac{\lambda^{2}B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}{2}=\frac{\lambda B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}{2}.

Choosing
\lambda=\frac{\sqrt{2L\log 2}}{B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}}
and using the above inequality, we get that Eq. (
19
) can be upper bounded as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{\lambda}\log\left(2^{L}\cdot\mathbb{E}\exp\lambda Z\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}Z+\sqrt{2L\log 2}B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{2L\log 2}+1)B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}.

Further applying Lemma
29
completes the proof.
∎

C.2
Adversarial Generalization Bound for t-NNs

Proof of Theorem
5
.

According to Theorem 2 and Eq. (4) in Ref.
[
3
]
, the adversarial generalization gap of
\ell\circ f
for any
f\in\mathfrak{F}
with
L_{\ell}
-Lipschitz continuous loss function
\ell
satisfying Assumption
2
can be upper bounded by
L_{\ell}\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}})
, where
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}})
is the empirical Rademacher complexity of the adversarial version
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
of the function set
\mathfrak{F}
defined as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}:=\{\tilde{f}:(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)\mapsto\min_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yf(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ f\in\mathfrak{F}\}.

(21)

To bound
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}})
, we use the Dudley’s inequality (Lemma
31
) which requires to compute the covering number of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
.

Let
\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}
be the
\delta_{l}
-covering of
\{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{l}\},\penalty\ \forall l=1,\cdots,L
. Consider the following subset of
\mathfrak{F}
whose t-matrix weights are all in
\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}
:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathfrak{F}_{c}:=\left\{f_{c}:\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}\mapsto f_{\mathcal{W}_{c}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})\penalty\ |\penalty\ \mathcal{W}_{c}=(\textnormal{{w}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}_{c},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{c}),\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}\right\}

with adversarial version

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}:=\left\{\tilde{f}_{c}:(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)\mapsto\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yf_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\penalty\ |\penalty\ f_{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{c}\right\}.

For all
\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
, we need to find the smallest distance to
\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}
, i.e. we need to calculate

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\inf_{\tilde{f}_{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}_{c}}\right\|_{S}.

For all
(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\in S
, given
\tilde{f}
and
\tilde{f}_{c}
with
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{c}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\delta_{l},\penalty\ l=1,\cdots,L
, consider

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{f}_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|

\displaystyle=\left|\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})-\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})\right|.

Letting
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
and
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{f}_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})|.

Let

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}=\left\{\begin{aligned} \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{if}\quad y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})\geq y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})\\
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{if}\quad y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})<y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})\end{aligned}\right..

Then,

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{f}_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})|\leq|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|=|f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

Let
{g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})=\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}\bigg(\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L-1)}_{c}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})\cdots)))\bigg)
and
g_{0}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})=f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}|g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

We can see that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l}B_{l}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi},

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))-\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

(22)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)})*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{l}\cdot\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l-1}B_{l^{\prime}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\delta_{l}\cdot B_{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{l^{\prime}\neq l}B_{l^{\prime}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}=\frac{\delta_{l}B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}},

where
B_{\tilde{f}}
is given in Lemma
39
.

Letting
\frac{\delta_{l}B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}=\frac{\epsilon}{L}
it gives

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\inf_{\tilde{f}_{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}_{c}}\right\|_{S}\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}|g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon.

Then,
\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}
is an
\epsilon
-covering of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
.
We further proceed by computing the
\epsilon
-covering number of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)\leq|\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}|=\prod_{l=1}^{L}|\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}|\leq\prod_{l=1}^{L}\left(\frac{3B_{l}}{\delta_{l}}\right)^{cd_{l-1}d_{l}}\leq\left(\frac{3LB_{\tilde{f}}}{\epsilon}\right)^{\sum_{l=1}^{L}cd_{l-1}d_{l}}.

where the second inequality is due to Lemma
32
.

Then, we use Dudley’s integral in Lemma
31
to upper bound
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}})
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}})

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\delta>0}\left(8\delta+\frac{12}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{\delta}^{{D}_{\tilde{f}}/2}\sqrt{\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}{\sf d}\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\delta>0}\left(8\delta+\frac{12}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{\delta}^{{D}_{\tilde{f}}/2}\sqrt{\big(\sum_{l=1}^{L}cd_{l-1}d_{l}\big)\log\big(3LB_{\tilde{f}}/(\epsilon)\big)}{\sf d}\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\delta>0}\left(8\delta+\frac{12{D}_{\tilde{f}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}cd_{l-1}d_{l}}}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{\delta/{D}_{\tilde{f}}}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log\big(3L/(2t)\big)}{\sf d}t\right),

where the diameter
{D}_{\tilde{f}}
of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
is given in Lemma
40
and we can find from Lemma
40
that
{D}_{\tilde{f}}=2B_{\tilde{f}}
in our setting.

Following Ref.
[
55
]
, let
\delta\rightarrow 0
, and use integration by part, we obtain

\int_{0}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log\big(3L/(2t)\big)}{\sf d}t\leq\sqrt{\log 3L}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}})\leq\frac{24B_{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}\bigg(\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}cd_{l-1}d_{l}}\bigg)B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}}{\sqrt{N}},

and the proof can be completed by using Lemma
29
.
∎

C.3
Generalization Bound under Exact Low-tubal-rank Parameterization

Proof of Theorem
6
.

The idea is similar to the proof of Theorem
5
. According to Theorem 2 and Eq. (4) in Ref.
[
3
]
, the adversarial generalization gap of
\ell\circ f
for any
f\in\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
with
L_{\ell}
-Lipschitz continuous loss function
\ell
satisfying Assumption
2
can be upper bounded by
L_{\ell}\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
, where
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
is the empirical Rademacher complexity of the adversarial version
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
of function set
\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
defined as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}:=\{\tilde{f}:(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)\mapsto\min_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yf(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ f\in\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}\}.

(23)

To bound
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
, we first use the Dudley’s inequality (Lemma
31
) and compute the covering number of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
.

Let
\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}
be the
\delta_{l}
-covering of
\{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{l}\penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ r_{\textnormal{t}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)})\leq r_{l}\},\penalty\ \forall l=1,\cdots,L
. Consider the following subset of
\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
whose t-matrix weights are all in
\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}
:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathfrak{F}_{c}:=\left\{f_{c}:\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}\mapsto f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{c})\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{c}=(\textnormal{{w}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}_{c},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{c}),\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}\right\}

with adversarial version

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}:=\left\{\tilde{f}_{c}:(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)\mapsto\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yf_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\penalty\ |\penalty\ f_{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{c}\right\}.

For all
\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
, we need to find the smallest distance to
\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}
, i.e. we need to calculate

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}}\inf_{\tilde{f}_{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}_{c}}\right\|_{S}.

For all
(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\in S
, given
\tilde{f}
and
\tilde{f}_{c}
with
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{c}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\delta_{l},\penalty\ l=1,\cdots,L
, consider

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{f}_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|

\displaystyle=\left|\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})-\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})\right|.

Letting
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
and
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{f}_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})|.

Let

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}=\left\{\begin{aligned} \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{if}\quad y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})\geq y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})\\
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{if}\quad y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})<y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{c}_{i})\end{aligned}\right.

Then,

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{f}_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})|\leq|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-y_{i}f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|=|f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

Let
{g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})=\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}\bigg(\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L-1)}_{c}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})\cdots)))\bigg)
and
g_{0}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})=f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\delta})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-f_{c}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}|g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

We can see that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l}B_{l}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))-\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)})*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{c}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{l}\cdot\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l-1}B_{l^{\prime}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\delta_{l}\cdot B_{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{l^{\prime}\neq l}B_{l^{\prime}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}=\frac{\delta_{l}B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}.

Letting
\frac{\delta_{l}B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}=\frac{\epsilon}{L}
gives

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}}\inf_{\tilde{f}_{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{f}_{c}}\right\|_{S}\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}|g_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon.

Then,
\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}
is an
\epsilon
-covering of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
.
We further proceed by computing the
\epsilon
-covering number of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)\leq|\mathfrak{F}_{c}^{\textnormal{adv}}|=\prod_{l=1}^{L}|\mathbf{\mathsf{C}}_{l}|\overset{(i}{\leq}\prod_{l=1}^{L}\left(\frac{9B_{l}}{\delta_{l}}\right)^{{\sf c}r_{l}(d_{l-1}+d_{l}+1)}\leq\left(\frac{9LB_{\tilde{f}}}{\epsilon}\right)^{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\sf c}r_{l}(d_{l-1}+d_{l}+1)},

where the inequality
(i)
holds due to Lemma
33
.

Then, we use Dudley’s integral to upper bound
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{R}_{S}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\delta>0}\left(8\delta+\frac{12}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{\delta}^{{D}_{\tilde{f}}/2}\sqrt{\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}{\sf d}\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\delta>0}\left(8\delta+\frac{12}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{\delta}^{{D}_{\tilde{f}}/2}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\sf c}r_{l}(d_{l-1}+d_{l}+1)\log\big(9LB_{\tilde{f}}/\epsilon\big)}{\sf d}\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\delta>0}\left(8\delta+\frac{12{D}_{\tilde{f}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\sf c}r_{l}(d_{l-1}+d_{l}+1)}}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{\delta/{D}_{\tilde{f}}}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log\big(9L/(2t)\big)}{\sf d}t\right),

where the diameter
{D}_{\tilde{f}}
of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
is given in Lemma
40
and we have
{D}_{\tilde{f}}=2B_{\tilde{f}}
.
Following Ref.
[
55
]
, let
\delta\rightarrow 0
, and use interation by part, we obtain
\int_{0}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log\big(9L/(2t)\big)}{\sf d}t\leq\sqrt{\log 9L}.
Further applying Lemma
29
completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Implicit bias towards low-rankness in the transformed domain

Recent research has shown that GF maximizes the margin of homogeneous networks during standard training, which leads to an implicit bias towards margin maximization
[
30
,
16
]
. Moreover, it has been demonstrated that this implicit bias also extends to adversarial margin maximization during adversarial training of multi-homogeneous fully connected neural networks with exponential loss
[
29
]
. Our analysis builds on these findings by showing that this implicit bias also holds for adversarial training of t-NNs when the adversarial perturbation is scale invariant
[
29
]
.

First, it is straightforward to see that any t-NN
f\in\mathfrak{F}
is homogeneous as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=a^{L+1}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}),

(24)

for any positive constant
a
.

Lemma 18

(Euler’s theorem on t-NNs)
.

For any t-NN
f\in\mathfrak{F}
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\langle{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=(L+1)f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}).

(25)

Proof.

By taking derivatives with respect to
a
on both sides of Eq. (
24
), we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\langle{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}},\frac{{\sf d}(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}{{\sf d}a}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=(L+1)a^{L}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}),

which immediately results in Eq. (
25
).
∎

In this section, we follow the setting of Ref.
[
29
]
where the adversarial perturbation
\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}
is scale invariant.
As Lemma
7
shows,
l_{2}
-FGM
[
34
]
, FGSM
[
9
]
,
l_{2}
-PGD and
l_{\infty}
-PGD
[
31
]
perturbations for the t-NNs are all scale invariant.

Proof of Lemma
7
.

Note that by taking derivatives with respect to
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}
on both sides of Eq. (
24
), we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}}=a^{L+1}{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}},

(26)

Therefore, any
{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}}
is positive homogeneous. Then, for any non-zero
\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}={\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}}
, we prove Lemma
7
in the following cases:

•

l_{2}
-FGM perturbtion
[
34
]
. The
l_{2}
-FGM perturbtion is defined as

\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{\textnormal{FGM}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})={\xi y{\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}}{\left\|{y\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{-1}}=-{\xi y\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}{\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{-1}},

because
\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}\leq 0
. Using Eq. (
26
), we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{\textnormal{FGM}}(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=-\frac{\xi y\cdot a^{L+1}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}{\left\|{a^{L+1}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}=\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{\textnormal{FGM}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}).

•

FGSM perturbations
[
9
]
.
The FGSM perturbtion is taken as

{\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{\textnormal{FSGM}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=\xi\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{sgn}}}}}}}(\xi y{\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}).

Using Eq. (
26
), we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{\textnormal{FSGM}}(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=\xi\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{sgn}}}}}}}(\xi y{\tilde{\ell}^{\prime}a^{L+1}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}})=\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{\textnormal{FSGM}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}).

•

l_{2}
-PGD perturbation
[
31
]
.
The
l_{2}
-PGD perturbtion is taken as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j+1}_{\textnormal{PGD}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(0,\xi)}\left[\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j}_{\textnormal{PGD}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})-\rho\frac{\xi y\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}{\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right],

(27)

where
j
is the attack step,
\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(\xi)}
is the projector onto
l_{2}
-norm ball of radius
\xi
, and
\rho
is the learning rate.
We prove by induction. For
j=0
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{1}_{\textnormal{PGD}}(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(0,\xi)}\left[-\rho\frac{\xi ya^{L+1}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}{\left\|{a^{L+1}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right]=\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{1}_{\textnormal{PGD}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}).

If we have
\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j}_{\textnormal{PGD}}(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j}_{\textnormal{PGD}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
, then for
j+1
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j+1}_{\textnormal{PGD}}(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(0,\xi)}\left[\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j}_{\textnormal{PGD}}(a{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})-\rho\frac{\xi ya^{L+1}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}{\left\|{a^{L+1}\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right]

(28)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{2}(0,\xi)}\left[\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j}_{\textnormal{PGD}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})-\rho\frac{\xi y\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}{\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{z}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}^{j+1}_{\textnormal{PGD}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}).

•

l_{\infty}
-PGD perturbation
[
31
]
. Since the scale invariance of this pertubation can be proved very similarly to that of
l_{2}
-PGD perturbations, we just omit it.

∎

For an original example
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}
, the margin for its adversarial example
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
is defined as

\tilde{q}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}):=y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}});{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
; for sample
S=\{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}
, the margin for the
N
corresponding examples is denoted by
\tilde{q}_{m}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
where
m\in\mathop{\Argmin}_{m=1,\cdots,N}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}):{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
.

Let
\rho=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}
for simplicity. We use the normalized parameter
\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}:={\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}/\rho
to denote the direction of the weights
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}
.
We introduce the normalized margin of
(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})
as
\hat{q}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}):=\tilde{q}_{i}(\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})=\tilde{q}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})\rho^{-(L+1)}
, and similarly define the normalized robust margin of the sample
S
as
\hat{q}_{m}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}):=\tilde{q}_{m}(\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})=\tilde{q}_{m}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})\rho^{-(L+1)}
.

Note that the adversarial empirical risk can be written as
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}))}
.
Motivated by
[
30
]
which uses the LogSumExp function to smoothly approximate the normalized standard margin, we define the smoothed normalized margin
\tilde{\gamma}
as follows.

Definition 11

(Smoothed normalized robust margin)
.

For a loss function
8
8

8

In this paper, the loss function satisfying Assumption
2
belongs to the class of

margin-based loss function

[
42
, Definition 2.24]
. That is, although the loss function
\ell(f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)
is a binary function of
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})
and
y
, there is a unary function
\mathfrak{l}(\cdot)
, such that
\ell(f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)=\mathfrak{l}(yf(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))
. With a slight abuse of notation, we simply use
\ell^{-1}(\cdot)
to denote
\mathfrak{l}^{-1}(\cdot)
, i.e., if
z=\ell(f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)
then we have
\ell^{-1}(z)=yf(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})
.

\ell
satisfying Assumption
2
, the smoothed normalized robust margin is defined as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \tilde{\gamma}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}):=\frac{\ell^{-1}(N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}})}{\rho^{L+1}}=\frac{{\mathfrak{g}}(\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}{\rho^{L+1}}=\frac{{\mathfrak{g}}\left(-\log\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}))}\right)\right)}{\rho^{L+1}}.

(29)

To better understand the relation between the normalized sample robust margin
\hat{q}_{m}
and the smoothed normalized robust margin
\tilde{\gamma}
, we provide the following lemma.

Lemma 19

(Adapted from Lemma A.5 of Ref.
[
30
]
)
.

Under Assumption
2
, we have the following properties about the robust marin
\tilde{q}_{m}
:

(a)

{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{m})-\log N\leq\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\leq{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{m})
.

(b)

If
\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}>{\mathfrak{f}}(b_{{\mathfrak{f}}})
, then there exists
\xi\in\left({\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{m})-\log N,{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{m})\right)\cap(b_{{\mathfrak{f}}},\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{q}_{m}-\rho^{-(L+1)}{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\xi)\log N\leq\tilde{\gamma}\leq\hat{q}_{m},

which shows the smoothed normalized margin
\tilde{\gamma}
is a rough approximation of the normalized robust margin
\hat{q}_{m}
.

(c)

For a sequence
\{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{s}\penalty\ |\penalty\ s\in\mathbb{N}\}
, if
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{s})\rightarrow 0
,
then

\left|\tilde{\gamma}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{s})-\hat{q}_{m}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{s})\right|\rightarrow 0
.

D.1
Convergence to KKT points of Euclidean norm minimization in direction

The KKT condition for the optimization problem Eq. (
14
) are

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}{\frac{\partial{\hat{q}_{i}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{0}},

(30)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(\tilde{q}_{i}-1)

\displaystyle=0,\quad i=1,\cdots,N,

where the dual variables
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
.
We define the approximate KKT point in a similar manner to Ref.
[
29
]
as follows.

Definition 12

(Approximate KKT points)
.

The
(\kappa,\iota)
-approximate KKT points of the
optimization problem are those feasible points which satisfy the following two conditions:

\displaystyle\penalty

\displaystyle\textnormal{Condition (I):}\qquad

\displaystyle\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}{\frac{\partial{\tilde{q}_{i}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa,

(31)

\displaystyle\textnormal{Condition (II):}\qquad

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(\tilde{q}_{i}-1)

\displaystyle\leq\iota,\penalty\ \penalty\ i=1,\cdots,N,

where
\kappa,\iota>0
and
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
.

Proof of Lemma
9
.

Let
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}:={\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}/\tilde{q}_{m}^{\frac{1}{L+1}}
denote the scaled version of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)
such that the sample robust margin
\tilde{q}_{m}=1
. Thus we have
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=\tilde{q}_{m}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})
by homogeneity of t-NNs. According to Lemma
18
, we further have

\displaystyle\penalty\ (L+1)f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=\left\langle{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}},{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\rangle,\quad(L+1)f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})=\left\langle\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}},{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})}}{\partial\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}\right\rangle,

leading to

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\langle\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}},{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})}}{\partial\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{m}^{L/(L+1)}}{\frac{\partial{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}.

We will prove that
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}
is a
(\kappa,\iota)
-KKT point of Problem (
14
) with
(\kappa,\iota)\rightarrow\textnormal{{0}}
.

Let
\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(t):={\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)}}{{\sf d}t}}
for simplicity. By the chain rule and GF update rule, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i}){\frac{\partial{{\tilde{q}_{i}}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}.

Using the homogeneity of t-NNs, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{2}{\frac{{\sf d}{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}}}{{\sf d}t}}=\left\langle\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i}){\frac{\partial{{\tilde{q}_{i}}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}\right\rangle=(L+1)\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})\tilde{q}_{i}.

By letting
\nu(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})\tilde{q}_{i}
, we obtain
\left\langle\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}\right\rangle=(L+1)\nu/N
.

We construct the dual variables
\lambda_{i}
in Problem (
31
) in terms of
\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lambda_{i}(t):=\frac{1}{N}\tilde{q}^{1-2/(L+1)}_{m}\cdot\rho e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{-1}.

(32)

To prove
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}
is a
(\kappa,\iota)
-KKT point of Problem (
14
), we need to check the conditions in Problem (
31
).

Step 1: Check Condition (I) of the
(\kappa,\iota)
-approximate KKT conditions
. We check the Condition (I) in Problem (
31
) for all
t>t_{0}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}{\frac{\partial{\tilde{q}_{i}(\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})}}{\partial\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\left\|{\frac{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}{\tilde{q}_{m}^{{1}/{(L+1)}}}-\frac{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\cdot\frac{\rho}{\tilde{q}_{m}^{{1}/{(L+1)}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

(33)

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho^{2}}{\tilde{q}_{m}^{2/{(L+1)}}}\left\|{\frac{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}-\frac{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}^{2/(L+1)}}\left(2-2\left\langle\frac{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}},\frac{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\frac{2}{\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})^{2/(L+1)}}\left(1-\left\langle\frac{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}},\frac{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle:=\kappa^{2}(t),

where equality
(i)
is obtained by using the definition that
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}={\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}/\tilde{q}_{m}^{\frac{1}{L+1}}
, the fact
\tilde{q}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}});{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})=y_{i}\cdot\tilde{q}_{m}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}(\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}});\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})=\tilde{q}_{i}(\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})
due to the scale invariance of the adversarial perturbation
\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
and the homogeneity of t-NN, and the chain rule in computing
{\frac{\partial{\tilde{q}_{i}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}{\frac{\partial{\tilde{q}_{i}(\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})}}{\partial\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\hat{q}_{m}^{\frac{L}{L+1}}}{\frac{\partial{\tilde{q}_{i}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}=\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{-1}\frac{\rho}{\tilde{q}_{m}^{\frac{L}{L+1}}}\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}{e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\frac{\partial{\tilde{q}_{i}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}=\frac{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\frac{\rho}{\tilde{q}_{m}^{\frac{L}{L+1}}}.

In Eq. (
33
), equality

holds by property

in Lemma

;
(iii)
holds by the non-decreasing property of
\tilde{\gamma}(t)
for all

in Lemma

.

Note that Eq. (

) indicates that
\kappa(t)
is in terms of the cosine of the angle between
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)
and
\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(t)
.
We can further obtain that

and

which was orignially observed by Ref.

for standard training

{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}
.
on a fixed training sample

.

Lemma 20

(Adapted from Lemma C.12 in Ref.
[

]
)
.

Under Assumption

and Assumption

for t-NNs, the angle between
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\left\langle\frac{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)}{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}},\frac{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(t)}{\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(t)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right\rangle\rightarrow 1.

Note that according to Assumption

we have
\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})\geq b_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\rho(t_{0})^{-(L+1)}
which means

cannot be arbitrarily close to

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\kappa^{2}(t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2}{\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})^{2/(L+1)}}\left(1-\left\langle\frac{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}},\frac{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right\rangle\right)=0.

(34)

Step 2: Check Condition (II) of the
(\kappa,\iota)
-approximate KKT conditions

) for all

as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}(\tilde{q}_{i}(\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})-1)

\displaystyle=\rho\left\|{\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{-1}\tilde{q}_{m}^{-2/(L+1)}\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})(\tilde{q}_{i}-\tilde{q}_{m}),

To upper bound Eq. (

Combing Eq. (

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i}\lambda_{i}(\tilde{q}_{i}(\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})-1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K\tilde{q}_{m}^{-2/(L+1)}\rho^{2}}{(L+1)\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{m})-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})(\tilde{q}_{i}-\tilde{q}_{m})

(37)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\geq}\frac{2K\tilde{\gamma}^{-2/(L+1)}}{(L+1)\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{m})-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})(\tilde{q}_{i}-\tilde{q}_{m}),

by Lemma

.

In Eq. (

), if
\tilde{q}_{i}>\tilde{q}_{m}
, then there exists an
\xi_{i}\in(\tilde{q}_{m},\tilde{q}_{i})

{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{m})-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})={\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\xi_{i})(\tilde{q}_{i}-\tilde{q}_{m})

{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i})\leq K^{\lceil\log_{2}(\tilde{q}_{i}/\xi_{i})\rceil}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\xi_{i})

\lceil\log_{2}(\tilde{q}_{i}/\xi_{i})\rceil\leq\log_{2}(2B_{0}\rho^{L+1}/\tilde{q}_{m})\leq\log_{2}(2B_{0}/\tilde{\gamma})
, where

\displaystyle\penalty\ B_{0}(t):

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{{\tilde{q}_{i}}{\rho^{-(L+1)}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}\neq\textnormal{{0}}\right\}=\sup\left\{\tilde{q}_{i}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=1\right\}.

Then, for all
t>t_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i}\lambda_{i}(\tilde{q}_{i}(\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})-1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K\tilde{\gamma}^{-2/(L+1)}\rho^{2}}{(L+1)\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}K^{\log_{2}(2B_{0}/\tilde{\gamma})}\sum_{i:\tilde{q}_{i}\neq\tilde{q}_{m}}e^{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\xi_{i})(\tilde{q}_{i}-\tilde{q}_{m})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\xi_{i})(\tilde{q}_{i}-\tilde{q}_{m})

(38)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{2K\tilde{\gamma}^{-2/(L+1)}}{(L+1)\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}K^{\log_{2}(2B_{0}/\tilde{\gamma})}\cdot Ne

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{2KNe\tilde{\gamma}^{-2/(L+1)}}{(L+1)\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}\left(\frac{B_{0}}{\tilde{\gamma}}\right)^{\log_{2}(2K)}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\frac{2KNe}{(L+1)\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})^{-2/(L+1)}}\left(\frac{B_{0}}{\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})}\right)^{\log_{2}(2K)}\cdot\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}

\displaystyle:=\iota(t),

where

holds because the function

on

has maximum

at

;

is due to
a^{\log_{c}b}=b^{\log_{c}a}

in Lemma

.
Note that by Lemma

, we have

, which further yields

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\iota(t)=0.

(39)

Step 3: Check the condition for convergence to KKT point
.
According to Eq. (
34
) and Eq. (
39
), the limit point of
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(t)
satisfy the
(\kappa,\iota)
-approximate KKT conditions of Problem
14
along the trajectory
of adversarial training of t-NN with scale invariant adversarial perturbations where
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}(\kappa(t),\iota(t))=\textnormal{{0}}
. Then, we need to check the condition between
(\kappa,\iota)
-approximate points and KKT points.

According to Ref.
[
30
]
, the KKT condition becomes a necessary condition for global optimality of Problem (
14
) when the Mangasarian-Fromovitz
Constraint Qualification (MFCQ)
[
33
]
is satisfied.
It is straightforward to see that Problem (
14
) satisfies the MFCQ condition, i.e.,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\langle{\frac{\partial{\tilde{q}_{i}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}\right\rangle=(L+1)\tilde{q}_{i}\geq 0.

at every feasible point
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}
.
Then restating the theorem in Ref.
[
7
]
regarding the relation between
(\kappa,\iota)
-approximate KKT
point and KKT point in our setting yields the following result.

Theorem 21

(Theorem 3.6 in Ref.
[
7
]
and Theorem C.4 in Ref.
[
30
]
)
.

Let

\{\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(j)\penalty\ |\penalty\ j\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of feasible point of Problem (
14
), and
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(j)
is a
(\kappa(j),\iota(j))
-approximate KKT point for all
j
with two sequences
10
10

10

Using the same method to
[
30
, Lemma C.12]
, we can construct the two sequences, i.e.,
\{\kappa(j)>0\penalty\ |\penalty\ j\in\mathbb{N}\}
and
\{\iota(j)>0\penalty\ |\penalty\ j\in\mathbb{N}\}
, based on Eq. (
33
) and Eq. (
35
).

\{\kappa(j)>0\penalty\ |\penalty\ j\in\mathbb{N}\}
and
\{\iota(j)>0\penalty\ |\penalty\ j\in\mathbb{N}\}
and
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(\kappa(j),\iota(j))=\textnormal{{0}}
. If
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}(j)=\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}^{*}
, and MFCQ holds at
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}^{*}
, then
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}^{*}
is a KKT point of Problem (
14
).

Recall that
\check{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}={\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}/\tilde{q}_{m}^{\frac{1}{L+1}}
. Then, it can be concluded that the limit point of
\{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)/\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}:t>0\}
of GF for empirical adversarial risk
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}});{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}))}
with scale invariant perturbations
\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}
is aligned with the direction of a KKT point of Problem (
14
).
∎

D.2
Technical Lemmas for Proving Lemma
9

Lemma 22
.

Under Assumption
2
, we have the following statements for GF-based adversarial training in Eq. (
13
) with scale invariant perturbations:

(\textnormal{{I}})
.

For a.e.
t\in(t_{0},\infty)
, the smoothed normalized robust margin
\tilde{\gamma}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t))
defined in Eq. (
29
) is non-decreasing, i.e.,

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{{\sf d}{\tilde{\gamma}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t))}}{{\sf d}t}}\geq 0.

(\textnormal{{I}}\textnormal{{I}})
.

The adversarial objective
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}});{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}))}
with scale invariant pertubations
\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i}
converges to zero as
t\rightarrow\infty
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t))=0,

(40)

and the Euclidean norm of the t-NN weights diverges, i.e.,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}(t)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\infty.

(41)

Proof of Lemma
22
.

We follow the idea of Ref.
[
30
]
to prove Lemma
22
as follows.

Step 1: Prove Part (I)
. We prove
(I)
by showing the following results for all
t\geq t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{{\sf d}{\log\rho}}{{\sf d}t}}>0\quad\textnormal{and}\quad{\frac{{\sf d}{\log\tilde{\gamma}}}{{\sf d}t}}\geq(L+1)\left({\frac{{\sf d}{\log\rho}}{{\sf d}t}}\right)^{-1}\left\|{{\frac{{\sf d}{\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}.

Recalling the quantity
\nu(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})\tilde{q}_{i}
, by chain rule we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{{\sf d}{\log\rho}}{{\sf d}t}}=\frac{1}{2\rho^{2}}{\frac{{\sf d}{\rho^{2}}}{{\sf d}t}}=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\left\langle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i}){\frac{\partial{{\tilde{q}_{i}}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}},{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}\right\rangle=\frac{\nu(L+1)}{\rho^{2}N}\overset{(i)}{\geq}0,

where
(i)
holds due to Lemma
23
.

By the chaining rule, we also have

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{{\sf d}{\log\tilde{\gamma}}}{{\sf d}t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\sf d}}{{\sf d}t}\left(\log\left(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\right)-(L+1)\log\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}{{\mathfrak{g}}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}\cdot\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\cdot\left(-{\frac{{\sf d}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}{{\sf d}t}}\right)-(L+1)^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\cdot\left(-{\frac{{\sf d}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}{{\sf d}t}}\right)-(L+1)^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\cdot\left(-{\frac{{\sf d}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}{{\sf d}t}}-(L+1)^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right).

Note that according to Eq. (
13
), we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{{\sf d}{\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}{{\sf d}t}}=\left\langle{\frac{\partial{\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}{\partial{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}},{\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}\right\rangle=-\left\|{{\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2},

for
t\geq 0
almost everywhere.
One the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ L\nu(t)=\left\langle{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}},{\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}\right\rangle.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle{{{\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{{\sf d}{\log\tilde{\gamma}}}{{\sf d}t}}\geq\frac{1}{N\nu}\left(\left\|{{\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}-\left\langle\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}},{\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}\right\rangle^{2}\right)\geq\frac{1}{N\nu(t)}\left\|{(\textnormal{{I}}-\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})^{\top})\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}({\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}})}\right\|^{2}.

By the chain rule,

\displaystyle{{{\displaystyle\penalty\ {\frac{{\sf d}{\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}=\frac{1}{\rho}(\textnormal{{I}}-\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}})^{\top})\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}({\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}),

for
t>0
allmost everywhere.
So, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{{\sf d}}{{\sf d}t}\log\tilde{\gamma}\geq\frac{\rho^{2}}{N\nu(t)}\left\|{{\frac{{\sf d}{\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}=(L+1)\left(\frac{{\sf d}}{{\sf d}t}\log\rho\right)^{-1}\left\|{{\frac{{\sf d}{\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\geq 0.

Step 2: Prove Part (II)
. Motivated by
[
30
, Lemma B.8]
, we prove
(II)
as follows. First, note that

\displaystyle\penalty\ -{\frac{{\sf d}{\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}{{\sf d}t}}=\left\|{{\frac{{\sf d}{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}}{{\sf d}t}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\geq\left\langle\hat{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}},\dot{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\rangle^{2}=\rho^{-2}N^{-2}(L+1)^{2}\nu^{2}.

By lower bounding
\nu
with Lemma
23
and replacing
\rho
with
({\mathfrak{g}}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})/\tilde{\gamma})^{1/(L+1)}
by the definition smoothed normalized robust margin of
\tilde{\gamma}
in Eq. (
29
), we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ -{\frac{{\sf d}{\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}}{{\sf d}t}}

\displaystyle\geq(L+1)^{2}\left(\frac{{\mathfrak{g}}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{{\mathfrak{g}}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}\right)^{2/(L+1)}

\displaystyle\geq(L+1)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})^{2/(L+1)}\cdot\frac{{\mathfrak{g}}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})^{2-2/(L+1)}}{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})^{2}}\cdot(N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}})^{2},

where the last inequality holds due to the non-decreasing property of
\tilde{\gamma}
in Part
(\textnormal{{I}})
. Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})^{2}}{{\mathfrak{g}}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})^{2-2/(L+1)}}\cdot\frac{{\sf d}}{{\sf d}t}\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\geq(L+1)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})^{2/(L+1)}.

By integrating on both sides from
t_{0}
to
t
, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ G\left(\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\right)\geq(L+1)^{2}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{0})^{2/(L+1)}(t-t_{0}),

(42)

where

\displaystyle\penalty\ G(z):=\int_{1/(N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(t_{0}))}^{z}\frac{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\log u)^{2}}{g(\log u)^{2-2/(L+1)}}{\sf d}u.

We use proof by contradiction to show the empirical training risk
\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}
converges to zero.
Note that
\frac{1}{(N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}})}
is non-decreasing. If
\frac{1}{(N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}})}
does not grow to
\infty
, then neither does
G(\frac{1}{(N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}})})
. But the RHS of Eq. (
42
) grows to
\infty
, which is a contradiction. Therefore,
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}=0
.
Hence

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(t)=0

and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho(t)=\infty
.
∎

Lemma 23

(Adapted from Lemma B.5 of Ref.
[
30
]
)
.

The quantity
\nu=\sum_{i}e^{-{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})}{\mathfrak{f}}(\tilde{q}_{i})\tilde{q}_{i}
has a lower bound for all
t\in(t_{0},\infty)
,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \nu(t)\geq\frac{{\mathfrak{g}}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(\log\frac{1}{N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}})}N\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}.

(43)

Lemma 24

(Lemma D.1 of Ref.
[
30
]
)
.

For
{\mathfrak{f}}(\cdot)
and
{\mathfrak{g}}(\cdot)
in Assumption
2
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{{\mathfrak{g}}(x)}{{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}(x)}\in\left[\frac{1}{2K}x,2Kx\right],\forall x\in[b_{{\mathfrak{g}}},\infty)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\frac{{\mathfrak{f}}(q)}{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}(q)}\in\left[\frac{1}{2K}q,2Kq\right],\forall q\in[{\mathfrak{g}}(b_{{\mathfrak{g}}}),\infty).

D.3
Proof of Theorem
10

Note that the t-NN
g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})
with weights
{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}=({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(1)},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(J)},\textnormal{{v}})
in Theorem
6
has the following structure

\displaystyle\penalty\ g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=\textnormal{{v}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\textnormal{{g}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))

\displaystyle\textnormal{{g}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{g}}^{(J)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{J}\times 1\times{\sf c}}

\displaystyle\textnormal{{g}}^{(j)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(j)}*_{M}\textnormal{{g}}^{(j-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{j}\times 1\times{\sf c}},\penalty\ \forall j=1,\cdots,J

\displaystyle\textnormal{{g}}^{(0)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}.

Let
\alpha=(\frac{1}{B_{v}})^{\frac{L-J}{L+1}}
and
\beta=(\frac{1}{B_{v}})^{-\frac{J+1}{L+1}}
, where
L
is a sufficiently large integer greater than
J
. We then construct a t-NN
h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}})
of
L
t-product layers which perfectly realizes
g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})
. Specifically, we construct
h
with weights
{\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}}=({\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}}^{(1)},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}}^{(L)},\textnormal{{h}})
satisfying the following equation

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}}=(\underbrace{\alpha{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(1)},\cdots,\alpha{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(J)}}_{\textnormal{first}\penalty\ J\penalty\ \textnormal{t-product layers}},\underbrace{\beta{\underline{\textnormal{{I}}}},\cdots,\beta{\underline{\textnormal{{I}}}}}_{\textnormal{last}\penalty\ (L-J)\penalty\ \textnormal{t-product layers}},\textnormal{{v}}),

or more clearly

\displaystyle\penalty\ h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=\textnormal{{v}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\textnormal{{h}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))

\displaystyle\textnormal{{h}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{h}}^{(L)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{J}\times 1\times{\sf c}}

\displaystyle\textnormal{{h}}^{(l)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\sigma(\beta{\underline{\textnormal{{I}}}}*_{M}\textnormal{{h}}^{(l-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{J}\times 1\times{\sf c}},\penalty\ \forall l=J+1,\cdots,L

\displaystyle\textnormal{{h}}^{(j)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\sigma(\alpha{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(j)}*_{M}\textnormal{{h}}^{(j-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{j}\times 1\times{\sf c}},\penalty\ \forall j=1,\cdots,J

\displaystyle\textnormal{{h}}^{(0)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},

where
{\underline{\textnormal{{I}}}}
is the t-identity tensor.

It is easy to prove that for any input
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}
, the input and output of
g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})=h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}})
, it can also be proved that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yg(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})=\min_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yh(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime};{\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}}).

Therefore,
h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}})
can also robustly classify
(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
because
g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})
can robustly classify
(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
.

Then, we consider the class of over-parameterized t-NNs
\mathfrak{F}=\{f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})\}
defined in Eq. (
14
) with dimensionality of weight
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{l}\times d_{l-1}\times{\sf c}}
safisfying
d_{l}\gg\max_{j\leq J}\{m_{j}\}
for all
l=1,\cdots,L
. Specifically, we construct
f
with weights

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}=({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)},\textnormal{{w}}),

and structure

\displaystyle\penalty\ f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))

\displaystyle\textnormal{{f}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{f}}^{(L)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{L}\times 1\times{\sf c}}

\displaystyle\textnormal{{f}}^{(l)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\textnormal{{f}}^{(l-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{l}\times 1\times{\sf c}},\penalty\ \forall l=1,\cdots,L

\displaystyle\textnormal{{f}}^{(0)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})

\displaystyle=\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}.

Note that according to our construction there is a function
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{h})\in\mathfrak{F}
with weights

{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{h}=({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}_{h},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{h},\textnormal{{w}}_{h})
satisfying

\displaystyle\penalty\ ({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{h})_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} ({\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}}^{(l)})_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}&\quad\textnormal{if}\penalty\ i_{1}\leq m_{l},i_{2}\leq m_{l-1},l\leq J\\
({\underline{\textnormal{{H}}}}^{(l)})_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}&\quad\textnormal{if}\penalty\ i_{1}\leq m_{J},i_{2}\leq m_{J},l=J+1,\cdots,L\\
0\qquad&\quad\textnormal{otherwise}\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\penalty\ \textnormal{{w}}_{i}=\left\{\begin{aligned} \textnormal{{h}}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{if}\penalty\ i\leq{\sf c}m_{J}\\
0&\quad\textnormal{otherwise}\end{aligned}\right..

We can also see that
h^{\prime}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}})=h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}})
for any
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times 1\times{\sf c}}
and any
\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}
satisfying
R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\mathbf{\delta}})\leq\xi
. Thus, we can say that the weight
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{h}
of
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{h})
is a feasible solution to Problem (
14
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{h})\geq 1,\forall i=1,\cdots,N.

Now consider the optimal solution
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*}=({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(1)},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(L)},\underline{\textnormal{{w}}}^{*})
to Problem (
14
). Then according to the optimility of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*}
and the feasibility of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{h}
to Problem
14
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{h}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{2}\cdot B_{v}^{2}\cdot(J+1)+\beta^{2}\cdot({\sf c}m_{J})\cdot(L-J)

(44)

\displaystyle=B_{v}^{\frac{2(J+1)}{L+1}}\left(K+1+({\sf c}m_{J})(L-J)\right)

and

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}_{i};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*})\geq 1,\forall i=1,\cdots,N.

(45)

As there is an example
(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*},y^{*})
satisfying
\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq 1
in the training set
S=\{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d\times 1\times{\sf c}}\times\{\pm 1\}
. Then according to Eq. (
45
), we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ y^{*}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*})=y^{*}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*}+\textnormal{{0}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*})\geq\min_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\mathbf{\delta}})\leq\xi}y^{*}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*})\geq 1,

which means

\displaystyle\penalty\ 1\leq f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}\prod_{l=1}^{L}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\leq\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}\prod_{l=1}^{L}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{L+1}\left(\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\right)\right)^{1/(L+1)}

indicating that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{L+1}\left(\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\right)>1.

On the other hand, according to Eq. (
44
) and Lemma
25
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(1)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\cdots=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(L)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{(L+1)}B_{v}^{\frac{2(J+1)}{L+1}}\left(K+1+({\sf c}m_{J})(L-J)\right)\right)^{1/2}.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{L+1}\left(\frac{\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}}{\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}}+\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}}{\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\right)\geq\left(\frac{1}{B_{v}}\right)^{\frac{J+1}{L+1}}\sqrt{\frac{L+1}{(J+1)+({\sf c}m_{J})(L-J)}}.

Note that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\textnormal{{W}}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\left\|{\textnormal{{W}}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}=\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}^{*(l)}}\right\|,

where

\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}^{*(l)}
denotes the
M
-block-diagonal matrix of weight tensor
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}
.
Then, we have
