\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}^{*(l)}}\right\|}{\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}^{*(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}}\geq\left(1+\frac{1}{L}\right)\left(\frac{1}{B_{v}}\right)^{\frac{J+1}{L+1}}\sqrt{\frac{L+1}{(J+1)+({\sf c}m_{J})(L-J)}}-\frac{1}{L}.

Taking the reciprocal of both sides gives

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{L}{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\left(r_{\textnormal{{stb}}}(\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}^{*(l)})\right)^{-1/2}}\leq\frac{1}{\left(1+\frac{1}{L}\right)\left(\frac{1}{B_{v}}\right)^{\frac{J+1}{L+1}}\sqrt{\frac{L+1}{(J+1)+({\sf c}m_{J})(L-J)}}-\frac{1}{L}}.

Lemma 25
.

For every
1\leq i,j\leq L
, we have
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
1\leq i<j\leq L
. For
\mu>0
, we define a t-NN
f_{\mu}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})
with weights
{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}=({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(1)},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{L},\textnormal{{v}})
which are constructed from
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*})
whose weights
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*}=({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(1)},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(L)},\underline{\textnormal{{w}}}^{*})
is an optimal solution to Problem (
14
). Specifically, the construction of
{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}=({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(1)},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{L},\textnormal{{v}})
is given as follows:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \forall l=1,\cdots,L,\quad{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{(l)}=\left\{\begin{aligned} {\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(l)}&\quad\textnormal{if}\penalty\ l\neq i\penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ 1\neq j\\
\mu{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}&\quad\textnormal{if}\penalty\ l=i\\
\mu^{-1}{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}&\quad\textnormal{if}\penalty\ 1=j\end{aligned}.\right.

Note that for every input example
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}
and perturbation
\underline{\mathbf{\delta}}
,
f_{\mu}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}};{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}})=f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}+\underline{\mathbf{\delta}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*})
, then
{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}
is also feasible to Problem (
14
). Note that we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{{\sf d}}{{\sf d}\mu}\left(\left\|{\mu{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}+\left\|{\mu^{-1}{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\right)=2\mu\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}-2\mu^{-3}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}.

When
\mu=1
the above expression equals
2\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}-2\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}
. Hence, if
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\neq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}
, then the derivative at
\mu
is non-zero, which leads to a contradiction to the optimality of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*}
to Problem (
14
).
Note that if we consider changing norms of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}
and
w
instead of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}
and
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}
, the same conclusion also holds.
Thus,
the optimality of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*}
strictly leads to
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(i)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{*(j)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}^{*}}\right\|_{2}
.
∎

Appendix E
Generalization bound of approximately low-tubal-rank t-NNs

Proof of Theorem
12
.

Given a
(\delta,\textnormal{{r}})
-approximately low-tubal-rank parameterized t-NN
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})\in\mathfrak{F}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
, let
g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\in\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
be its compressed version whose t-product layer weight tensors have tubal-ranks upper bounded by
r
.

Step 1: Upper bound the adversarial empirical
L_{2}
-distance between
f
and
g
.
Consider function
g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})=g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
parameterized by
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}=({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}_{r_{1}},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{r_{L}},\textnormal{{w}})
as the function whose t-product layer weights are low-tubal-rank approximations of
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
.
Let
\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)=\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yf(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})
and
\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)=\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yg(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})
denote the adversarial versions of
f
and
g
, respectively.

We first bound the adversarial empirical
L_{2}
-distance between
f
and
g
as follows.

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|

\displaystyle=|\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})-\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})|.

Letting
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
and
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
, we have

|\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})|.

By letting

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}=\left\{\begin{aligned} \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{if}\quad y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})\geq y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})\\
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{otherwise}\end{aligned}\right.,

we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})|\leq|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|=|f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

Let

{h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})=\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}\bigg(\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{r_{l}}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L-1)}_{r-1}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{r}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})\cdots)))\bigg)
and
h_{0}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})=g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}|h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-h_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

We can see that for any
l=1,\cdots,L
:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l-1}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l-1}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi},

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1-1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))-\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)})*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{l}\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-h_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq B_{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{j\neq l}B_{j}\delta B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}=\frac{\delta B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}.

This gives

\displaystyle\penalty\ |f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}|h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-h_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{\delta B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}.

Then, we can set

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}=\delta B_{\tilde{f}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}^{-1}.

(46)

Step 2: Divide and conquer the adversarial gap.

To upper bound the adversarial gap
\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})
of
f
by using the properties of its compressed version
g
, we first decompose the adversarial gap into three terms as follows

\displaystyle\penalty

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})

(47)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\big[(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})-\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({g}))-(\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g))\big]}_{\textnormal{{I}}}+\underbrace{\big(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g)\big)}_{\textnormal{{I}}\textnormal{{I}}}.

Step 2.1
: Upper bound
I
I
. We first consider the event
{\cal E}_{1}
in which term
I
I
is upper bounded with high probability. As
g\in\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
, the term
I
I
has already been upper bounded according to Theorem
6
as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \textnormal{{I}}\textnormal{{I}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}L_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{{\sf c}\sum_{l=1}^{L}r_{l}(d_{l-1}+d_{l})\log(9(L+1))}+3B\sqrt{\frac{t}{2N}},

(48)

with high probability
1-2e^{-t}
.

Step 2.2
: Upper bound
I
.
Note that term
I
can be written as

\displaystyle\penalty

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g)-(\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g))

(49)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)]\right).

Step (2.2.1)
: Characterize the concentration behavior of
\ell\circ\tilde{f}-\ell\circ\tilde{g}
.
Given a constant
{\mathfrak{r}}>0
, consider the event
{\cal E}_{2}({\mathfrak{r}})
in which
\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}
already holds with high probability.
Then, conditioned on Event
{\cal E}_{2}({\mathfrak{r}})
, by using the
L_{\ell}
-Lipschitz continuity of the loss function
\ell(\cdot,\cdot)
derived from Assumption
2
, it can be proved that
\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)
also has a small population
L_{2}
-norm with high probability.

Regarding Eq. (
49
) , it is natural to characterize the concentration behavior of centered random variable
\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)]
.

•

First, its variance under event
{\cal E}_{2}
can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Var}}}}}}\left(\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)]\right)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{Var}}}}}}\left(\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\mathbb{E}_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)}\left[(\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y))-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)))^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)}[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y))-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y))]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\mathbb{E}_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)}\left[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y))-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y))\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}L_{\ell}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)}\left[\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}L_{\ell}^{2}\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq L_{\ell}^{2}{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}

•

Second, we upper bound its
L_{\infty}
-norm. First, Lemma
39
indicates that for any
h\in\mathfrak{F}
with adversarial version
\tilde{h}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
, we have
\left\|{\tilde{h}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq B_{\tilde{f}}:=B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}
. Then, by
\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}\subset\mathfrak{F}
, we have
\left\|{\tilde{f}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq B_{\tilde{f}}
and
\left\|{\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq B_{\tilde{f}}
. Therefore, we can upper bound the
L_{\infty}
-norm of
\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)]
as follows

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\left\|{\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)]}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\left\|{\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}+\left\|{\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)]}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\left\|{\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}),y)}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}L_{\ell}\sup_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)}|y\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}))-y\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})|+\mathbb{E}[L_{\ell}\sup_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)}|y\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})-y\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})|]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}4L_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}.

Then, the Talagrand’s concentration inequality (Lemma
35
) yields that with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})-\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({g}))-(\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g))

\displaystyle\leq 2\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})-\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({g}))-(\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g))\right]}_{(\textnormal{{I}})}+\frac{\sqrt{2t}L_{\ell}{\mathfrak{r}}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{8tL_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{N}.

Then, by the the standard symmetrization argument
[
47
]
, we obtain an upper bound on term
(\textnormal{{I}})
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})-\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({g}))-(\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g))\right]

(50)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})_{i=1}^{N}}\mathbb{E}_{(\varepsilon_{i})_{i=1}^{N}}\sup_{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}}\left[\frac{1}{N}\varepsilon_{i}\left(\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i}))-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i}))\right)\right]

\displaystyle=2\Phi({\mathfrak{r}}),

where
\Phi({\mathfrak{r}})
is defined as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Phi({\mathfrak{r}}):=\bar{R}_{N}\left(\{\ell\circ\tilde{f}-\ell\circ\tilde{g}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ \tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\}\right).

Thus, there is a constant
C>0
such that for any
\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
satisfying
\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}
, it holds with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
that

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({f})-\mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}({g}))-(\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(g))\leq C\left(\Phi({\mathfrak{r}})+L_{\ell}{\mathfrak{r}}\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{tL_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{N}\right).

(51)

We denote the above event by
{\cal E}_{3}({\mathfrak{r}})
. Note that Event
{\cal E}_{3}({\mathfrak{r}})
is conditioned on Event
{\cal E}_{2}({\mathfrak{r}})
.

Step (2.2.2)
: Upper bound the probability of Event
{\cal E}_{2}({\mathfrak{r}}):=\left\{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right\}
.
We further bound the probability of Event
{\cal E}_{2}({\mathfrak{r}})
in which
\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}
holds. Generally speaking,
\tilde{f}
and
\tilde{g}
are date dependent and we can only bound the empirical
L_{2}
-distance between
them. However, the local Rademacher complexity is characterized by the population
L_{2}
-norm. Thus, we need to bound the population
L_{2}
-distance between

\tilde{f}
and
\tilde{g}
.
Motivated by
[
43
]
, we use the ratio type empirical process to bound the bound the population
L_{2}
-distance.

According to Assumption
11
, their exists a function
\phi:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \dot{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\leq\phi({\mathfrak{r}})\penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \phi(2{\mathfrak{r}})\leq 2\phi({\mathfrak{r}}),\penalty\ (\forall{\mathfrak{r}}>0).

Define the quantity

\Gamma({\mathfrak{r}}):=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{h}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}h^{2}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}):\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right].

Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \Gamma({\mathfrak{r}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{h}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}h^{2}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}):\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}2B_{\tilde{f}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{h}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}):\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}2B_{\tilde{f}}\dot{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\overset{(ii)}{\leq}2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}}),

where
(i)
is by the Talagrand’s contraction lemma (Lemma
30
).

We can verify that the square
h^{2}(\cdot)
of any function
h\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
satisfies

(i)

its
L_{\infty}
-norm is upper bouned by
B_{\tilde{f}}^{2}
, i.e.,
\left\|{h^{2}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}=\sup_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)}|h^{2}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)|\leq B_{\tilde{f}}^{2}
.

(ii)

its second-order moment satisfies

\mathbb{E}_{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y}\left[(h^{2}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)^{2})\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y}\left[B_{\tilde{f}}^{2}(h^{2}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y))\right]=B_{\tilde{f}}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y}\left[h^{2}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)\right]
.

Thus,
h^{2}
satisfy the conditions in Eq. (7.6) and Eq. (7.7) of
[
42
]
with parameters
B=B_{\tilde{f}}^{2},V=B_{\tilde{f}}^{2}
and
\vartheta=1
. Noting that we have upper bounded

\Gamma({\mathfrak{r}})
by
2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
,
then by the peeling trick
[
42
, Eq. (7.17)]
, we can show
for any
{\mathfrak{r}}>\inf\{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[h^{2}]}:h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}):\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\}
and
t>0
that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{h\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}}\frac{\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}-\left\|{h}\right\|_{S}^{2}}{\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}}\geq 8\frac{2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})}{{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}}+B_{\tilde{f}}\sqrt{\frac{2t}{{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}N}}+B_{\tilde{f}}^{2}\frac{2t}{{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}N}\right]\leq e^{-t}.

We further define a function

{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}={\mathfrak{r}}_{*}(t)
as

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathfrak{r}}_{*}(t):=\inf\left\{{\mathfrak{r}}>0\penalty\ \bigg|\penalty\ \frac{16B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})}{{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}}+B_{\tilde{f}}\sqrt{\frac{2t}{{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}N}}+B_{\tilde{f}}^{2}\frac{2t}{{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}N}\leq\frac{1}{2}\right\},

(52)

which is useful to bound
the ratio of the empirical
L_{2}
-norm and the population
L_{2}
-norm of
an elements
h\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
with probability at least
1-e^{t}
:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}-\left\|{h}\right\|_{S}^{2}}{\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}_{*}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\leq 2(\left\|{h}\right\|_{S}^{2}+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}_{*}).

Recalling that
\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{S}\leq\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}
, we obtain that the probability of Event
{\cal E}_{2}(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})
with
\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}}=\sqrt{2(\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{2}(t))}
is at least
1-e^{-t}
.

Step 2.3
: Combining Events
{\cal E}_{1},{\cal E}_{2}(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}}),{\cal E}_{3}(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})
. By combining Eqs. (
46
), (
47
), (
51
) and (
48
) along with their underlying events
{\cal E}_{1},{\cal E}_{2}(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}}),{\cal E}_{3}(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathcal{L}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\textnormal{adv}}(f)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}L_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{{\sf c}\sum_{l=1}^{L}r_{l}(d_{l-1}+d_{l})\log(9(L+1))}+B\sqrt{\frac{t}{2N}}

\displaystyle+C_{2}\left(\Phi(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})+L_{\ell}\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{tL_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{N}\right),

with probability at least
1-4e^{-t}
.
∎

E.1
Several Useful Results

According to Theorem
12
, it remains to upper bound
\Phi(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})
.
In this subsection, we derive upper bounds on
\Phi({\mathfrak{r}})
in terms of covering numbers of the considered function sets
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
and
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
.

Consider the supremum of the empirical
L_{2}
-norm of any function
(\tilde{f}-\tilde{g})\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
on sample
S=\{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}
when the population
L_{2}
-norm is bounded by a given radius
{\mathfrak{r}}>0
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \beta_{S}=\beta_{S}({\mathfrak{r}})=\sup\left\{\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{S}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}},\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}\right\}.

(53)

Recall that we have assumed
\dot{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\leq\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
. We now give an explict example of
\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
in terms of the sum of covering entropy of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
and
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}

\displaystyle\penalty

\displaystyle\dot{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})

(54)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{S}\hat{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}\left(\left\{(\tilde{f}-\tilde{g})\penalty\ \in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}),\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\inf_{a}\left[a+\mathbb{E}_{S}\int_{a}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}\left(\left\{(\tilde{f}-\tilde{g})\penalty\ \in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}),\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right\}\right),\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\frac{1}{N}+\mathbb{E}_{S}\int_{1/N}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}\left(\left\{(\tilde{f}-\tilde{g})\penalty\ \in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}),\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right\}\right),\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\leq}\frac{1}{N}+\mathbb{E}_{S}\int_{1/N}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)+\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon

\displaystyle=:\phi({\mathfrak{r}}),

where
(i)
is the definition of localized Rademacher complexity;
(ii)
is due to Dudley’s inequality (Lemma
31
);
(iii)
is obtained by letting
a=1/N
;
(iv)
holds by Lemma
34
.

Lemma 26
.

We can upper bound
\Phi({\mathfrak{r}})
by using
\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty

\displaystyle\Phi({\mathfrak{r}})\leq CL_{\ell}\phi({\mathfrak{r}}).

(55)

Proof.

Recall that
\Phi({\mathfrak{r}})
is the average Rademacher complexity of the function set

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\{\ell\circ\tilde{f}-\ell\circ\tilde{g}\penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}},\penalty\ \tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}\right\}.

As
\ell(\cdot)
is
L_{\ell}
-Lipschitz, we have for any functions
\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
satisfying
\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sup_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}}\left\|{\ell\circ\tilde{f}-\ell\circ\tilde{g}}\right\|_{S}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\Big(\ell(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\ell(\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\Big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\sup_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\Big(L_{\ell}(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i}))\Big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{}{=}\sup_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}}L_{\ell}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{N}\Big(\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\Big)^{2}}

\displaystyle=L_{\ell}\sup_{\tilde{f},\tilde{g}}\left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{S}

\displaystyle=L_{\ell}\beta_{S},

where
(i)
holds because the loss function
\ell
is
L_{\ell}
-Lischitz continous.

To bound
\Phi({\mathfrak{r}})
, we first bound the its empirical version
using the Dudley’s inequlity (Lemma
31
) up to a constant as follows

\displaystyle\penalty

\displaystyle\inf_{a>0}\left[a+\int_{a}^{L_{\ell}\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}\left(\{\ell\circ\tilde{f}-\ell\circ\tilde{g}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ \tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\}\right),\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon\right]

(56)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}{\frac{L_{\ell}}{N}}+\int_{L_{\ell}/N}^{L_{\ell}\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}\left(\{\ell\circ\tilde{f}-\ell\circ\tilde{g}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ \tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\}\right),\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}{\frac{L_{\ell}}{N}}+\int_{L_{\ell}/N}^{L_{\ell}\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}\left(\{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ \tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\}\right),\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/L_{\ell})}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}{\frac{L_{\ell}}{N}}+\int_{1/N}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}\left(\{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ \tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\tilde{g}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\penalty\ \left\|{\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\}\right),\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},t)}{N}}\penalty\ L_{\ell}{\sf d}t

\displaystyle=L_{\ell}\phi({\mathfrak{r}}),

where in
(i)
we let
a=L_{\ell}/N
;
(ii)
holds by the Lipschitzness of
\ell
and the definition of covering number; we use change of variable
t=\epsilon/L_{\ell}
in
(iii)
.

By taking expectations on the RHS of Eq. (
56
) with respect to the sample
S
, we obtain Eq. (
55
).

∎

To determine an appropriate radius of the population
L_{2}
-norm
\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}}=2\sqrt{\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}^{2}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{2}}
, we need to compute the value of
{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}
of satisfying Eq. (
52
).
Using Eq. (
54
), we show how to compute
\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
when the covering numbers of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
and
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
satisfy a special bound.

Lemma 27

(Adapted from Lemma 3 in Ref.
[
43
]
)
.

Suppose that the covering numbers of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
and
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
satisfy

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sup_{S}\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)+\sup_{S}\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)\leq a_{1}+a_{2}\log(\epsilon^{-1})+a_{3}\epsilon^{-2q}

(57)

for some
q\leq 1
.
Then, it holds that

(I)

The bound
\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
of the local Rademacher complexity
\dot{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
of radius
{\mathfrak{r}}
can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \phi({\mathfrak{r}})\leq C\max\bigg\{\frac{1}{N}

\displaystyle+B_{\tilde{f}}\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}+{\mathfrak{r}}\sqrt{\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}},

(58)

\displaystyle C_{q}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}+\big(\frac{a_{3}B_{\tilde{f}}^{1-q}}{N}\big)^{\frac{1}{1+q}}+{\mathfrak{r}}^{1-q}\sqrt{\frac{a_{3}}{N}}\bigg]\bigg\},

for a universal constant
C>0
and a constant
C_{q}>0
which only depends on
q\leq 1
.

(II)

In particular, the quantity
{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}(t)
satisying Eq. (
52
) can be upper bounded as

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{2}(t)\leq C\left[B_{\tilde{f}}\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}+\big(\frac{a_{3}}{N}\big)^{\frac{1}{1+q}}\left(B_{\tilde{f}}^{\frac{1-q}{1+q}}+1\right)+\frac{1+tB_{\tilde{f}}}{N}\right].

Proof of Lemma
27
.

According to Eq. (
54
) which gives
\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
and the definition of
\beta_{S}
in Eq. (
53
), we need to upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S}\int_{1/N}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)+\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{S}\int_{1/N}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log(\epsilon^{-1})+a_{3}\epsilon^{-2q}}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{S}\int_{1/N}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log(\epsilon^{-1})}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon+\mathbb{E}_{S}\int_{1/N}^{\beta_{S}}\sqrt{\frac{a_{3}\epsilon^{-2q}}{N}}\penalty\ {\sf d}\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\sqrt{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{S}\beta_{S}}+\frac{\sqrt{a_{3}}}{1-q}\mathbb{E}_{S}\beta_{S}^{1-q}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\underbrace{\sqrt{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}\sqrt{2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}}}_{\textnormal{{I}}}+\underbrace{\frac{\sqrt{a_{3}}}{1-q}\left(2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1-q}{2}}}_{\textnormal{{I}}\textnormal{{I}}}.

Hence, if
\textnormal{{I}}\geq\textnormal{{I}}\textnormal{{I}}
, then

\displaystyle\penalty\ \phi({\mathfrak{r}})

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{N}+\sqrt{\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}}+\sqrt{2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{N}+C^{2}B_{\tilde{f}}\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}+C{\mathfrak{r}}\sqrt{\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}}+\frac{\phi({\mathfrak{r}})}{2},

which leads to

\displaystyle\penalty\ \phi({\mathfrak{r}})\leq\frac{2C}{N}+2C^{2}B_{\tilde{f}}\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}+2C{\mathfrak{r}}\sqrt{\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}}.

If
\textnormal{{I}}<\textnormal{{I}}\textnormal{{I}}
, then by using Young’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ \phi({\mathfrak{r}})

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{1}{N}+\frac{\sqrt{a_{3}}}{1-q}\left(2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1-q}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{N}+C\left(q\left(\frac{c_{1}^{1-q}C^{2}a_{3}}{N(1-q)^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+q}}+(1-q)\frac{2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})}{c_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{a_{3}}{N(1-q)}{\mathfrak{r}}^{2(1-q)}}\right),

for any
c_{1}>0
. Thus, by taking
c_{1}=4C(1-q)B_{\tilde{f}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ \phi({\mathfrak{r}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C}{N}+2qC\left(\frac{(4C(1-q)B_{\tilde{f}})^{1-q}C^{2}a_{3}}{N(1-q)^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+q}}+2C\sqrt{\frac{a_{3}}{N(1-q)}{\mathfrak{r}}^{2(1-q)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C}{N}+\frac{2qC^{2}\cdot 4^{\frac{1-q}{1+q}}}{1-q}\left(\frac{B_{\tilde{f}}^{1-q}a_{3}}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+q}}+\frac{2C}{(1-q)}{\mathfrak{r}}^{(1-q)}\sqrt{\frac{a_{3}}{N}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{q}}{N}+C_{q}\left(\frac{B_{\tilde{f}}^{1-q}a_{3}}{N}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+q}}+C_{q}{\mathfrak{r}}^{(1-q)}\sqrt{\frac{a_{3}}{N}},

where
C_{q}>0
is a universal constant only depending on
q
.

Then, we obtain the bound on
\phi({\mathfrak{r}})
in Eq. (
58
).
The bound on
{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{2}
can be obtained by simple calculations based on Eqs. (
52
) and (
58
).
∎

Lemma 28
.

When the population
L_{2}
-norm of any function
h=\tilde{f}-\tilde{g}\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
is upper bounded by
{\mathfrak{r}}
, its squared empirical
L_{2}
-norm can be upper bouned as follows:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{E}_{S}\left[\sup_{h}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})^{2}\penalty\ \bigg|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right]\leq 2B_{\tilde{f}}\dot{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}.

(59)

Proof.

The squared empirical
L_{2}
-norm can be upper bouned based on the population
L_{2}
-norm as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S}\left[\sup\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})^{2}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right]

(60)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})}[h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)^{2}]\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right]+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}2\mathbb{E}_{S,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\left[\sup\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})^{2}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})\penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right]+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}2B_{\tilde{f}}\mathbb{E}_{S,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\left[\sup\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\varepsilon_{i}h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ h\in(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}):\left\|{h}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\right)\right]+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{}{=}2B_{\tilde{f}}\dot{R}_{{\mathfrak{r}}}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}-\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}2B_{\tilde{f}}\phi({\mathfrak{r}})+{\mathfrak{r}}^{2},

where
(i)
is due to the symmertrization argument
[
47
]
and
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
are
i.i.d.
Rademacher variables, and
(ii)
holds because of the contraction inequality (Lemma
30
).
∎

E.2
Adversarial Generalization Gap under Assumption
13

Proof of Theorem
14
.

In this situation, we can see that for any
1\leq r_{l}\leq\min\{d_{l},d_{l-1}\}
, we can approximate
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}
with
its optimal tubal-rank-
r_{l}
approximation
tensor
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}
according to
[
20
, Theorem 3.7]

and achieve the following approximation error bound on F-norm

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}\times_{3}\textnormal{{M}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}\times_{3}\textnormal{{M}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{\sf c}\sum_{j={r_{l}}+1}^{\min\{d_{l},d_{l-1}\}}\sigma_{j}^{2}({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}\times_{3}\textnormal{{M}})_{:,:,k}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{\sf c}\sum_{j={r_{l}}+1}^{\min\{d_{l},d_{l-1}\}}(V_{0}\cdot j^{-\alpha})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{\sf c}\frac{1}{2\alpha-1}V_{0}^{2}(r_{l}-1)^{1-2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}{\sqrt{\frac{{\sf c}}{2\alpha-1}}V_{0}r_{l}^{(1-2\alpha)/2}:=\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{F}}},

where
(i)
holds because of Lemma
41
.

We also have a specral norm bound for
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}
according to
[
20
, Theorem 3.7]
under Assumption
13
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\leq V_{0}(r_{l}+1)^{-\alpha}:={\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}},

and we also have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{{\sf c}}\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}\leq\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{F}}.

(61)

Consider function
g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})=g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
parameterized by
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}=({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}_{r_{1}},\cdots,{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{r_{L}},\textnormal{{w}})
as the function whose t-product layer weights are low-tubal-rank approximations of
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})
.
Let
\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)=\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yf(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})
and
\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)=\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yg(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})
denote the adversarial versions of
f
and
g
, respectively.

This helps us bounding
\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}
as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|

\displaystyle=|\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})-\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})|.

Letting
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
and
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i}=\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}_{i})\leq\xi}y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\prime})
, we have

|\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})|.

By letting

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}=\left\{\begin{aligned} \underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{if}\quad y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})\geq y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})\\
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i}&\quad\textnormal{otherwise}\end{aligned}\right.,

we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})-\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})|=|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{f}_{i})-y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{g}_{i})|\leq|y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-y_{i}g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|=|f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

Let

{h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})=\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}\bigg(\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}_{r_{l}}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L-1)}_{r-1}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l+1)}_{r}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})\cdots)))\bigg)
and
h_{0}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})=g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\xi})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}|h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-h_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|.

We can see that for any
l=1,\cdots,L
:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l-1}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l-1}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi},

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1-1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))-\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)})*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l-1)}*_{M}\cdots*_{M}\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(1)}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})\cdots)}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}\prod_{l^{\prime}=1}^{l}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l^{\prime})}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi},

where in
(i)
we relax the inequality by using the tensor spectral norm of insted of the F-norm of
{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}_{r_{l}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(l)}
which leads to a smaller upper bound.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-h_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq B_{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{j\neq l}B_{j}\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}=\frac{\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}.

This gives

\displaystyle\penalty\ |f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-g(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}|h_{l}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})-h_{l-1}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i}^{\xi})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}.

Then, we can set

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}=}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}B_{\tilde{f}}}{B_{l}}={V_{0}B_{\tilde{f}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{(r_{l}+1)^{-\alpha}}{B_{l}}}.

(62)

Then we can construct an
\epsilon
-cover of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
composed of
\tilde{g}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}_{\textnormal{{r}}})
by carefully setting the value of rank parameter
\textnormal{{r}}=(r_{1},\cdots,r_{L})^{\top}
according to the covering accuracy
\epsilon
. Directily setting
\frac{\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}{D}_{\tilde{f}}}{2B_{l}}=\epsilon/L
, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\ r_{l}=\min\left\{\left\lceil A_{l}\cdot\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}\right\rceil-1,d_{l},d_{l-1}\right\}\leq\min\left\{A_{l}\cdot\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}},d_{l},d_{l-1}\right\},

where

\displaystyle\penalty\ A_{l}=\Big(\frac{B_{l}}{LV_{0}B_{\tilde{f}}}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}.

Then, we obtain the covering entropy of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
by

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\log\prod_{l=1}^{L}\left(\frac{9B_{l}}{\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{F}}}\right)^{r_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1){\sf c}}

(63)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\log\prod_{l=1}^{L}\left(\frac{9B_{l}}{\sqrt{{\sf c}}\delta_{l}^{\textnormal{sp}}}\right)^{r_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1){\sf c}}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\sum_{l=1}^{L}r_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1){\sf c}\log(9LB_{\tilde{f}}/(\sqrt{{\sf c}}\epsilon))

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}\sum_{l}(A_{l}\epsilon^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}})(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1){\sf c}\left(\log(\epsilon^{-1})+\log(9LB_{\tilde{f}}/(\sqrt{{\sf c}}))\right),

where
(ii)
holds by Eq. (
61
).

Since
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}}\subset\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}\leq\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}r_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1){\sf c}\log(9LB_{\tilde{f}}/(\sqrt{{\sf c}}\epsilon))

To use Lemma
27
, we bound
\log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)+{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)
when
\epsilon\geq 2/N
as follows:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \log{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\delta,\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/(2))+{\sf N}(\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}_{\textnormal{{r}}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon/2)\leq a_{1}+a_{2}\log(\epsilon^{-1})+a_{3}\epsilon^{-{\frac{1}{\alpha}}},

where

\displaystyle\penalty\ a_{1}

\displaystyle=\log(9LB_{\tilde{f}}/\sqrt{{\sf c}})a_{2},

\displaystyle a_{2}

\displaystyle={\sf c}\sum_{l=1}^{L}r_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1),

\displaystyle a_{3}

\displaystyle=\left(\log N+\log(9LB_{\tilde{f}}/\sqrt{{\sf c}})\right){\sf c}\sum_{l=1}^{L}A_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1).

For simplicity, further let

\displaystyle\penalty\ E_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}\log N}{N}=\frac{{\sf c}\sum_{l=1}^{L}r_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1)}{N}\log(9NLB_{\tilde{f}}/\sqrt{{\sf c}}),

(64)

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{3}}{N}=\frac{{\sf c}\sum_{l=1}^{L}A_{l}(d_{l}+d_{l-1}+1)}{N}\log(9NLB_{\tilde{f}}/\sqrt{{\sf c}}).

Then, according to Lemma
27
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{2}(t)\leq C\left\{B_{\tilde{f}}E_{1}+\frac{1+tB_{\tilde{f}}}{N},\quad E_{2}^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}\left(B_{\tilde{f}}^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}+1\right)\right\},

(65)

which further leads to

\displaystyle\penalty

\displaystyle\Phi(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})+L_{\ell}\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{tL_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{N}

(66)

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}2L_{\ell}\phi(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})+2L_{\ell}(\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*})\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{tL_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{N}

\displaystyle\overset{}{\leq}C_{q}L_{\ell}\max\bigg\{\frac{1}{N}+B_{\tilde{f}}E_{1}+(\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*})\sqrt{E_{1}},\penalty\ E_{2}^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}B_{\tilde{f}}^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}+(\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}\sqrt{E_{2}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+2L_{\ell}(\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*})\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{tL_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{N}.

Note that

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*})\sqrt{E_{1}}=\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{E_{1}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}\sqrt{E_{1}}\leq\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{E_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}E_{1},

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*})^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}\sqrt{E_{2}}\leq\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}\sqrt{E_{2}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}\sqrt{E_{2}},

\displaystyle\penalty\ (\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}+{\mathfrak{r}}_{*})\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}\leq\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{1}{2}({\mathfrak{r}}_{*}^{2}+\frac{t}{N}).

Then by simple calculation, we have

\displaystyle\Phi(\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}})+L_{\ell}\dot{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{tL_{\ell}B_{\tilde{f}}}{N}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}C_{\alpha}L_{\ell}\bigg\{B_{\tilde{f}}E_{1}+\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{E_{1}}+E_{2}^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}\left(B_{\tilde{f}}^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha+1}}+1\right)+\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\frac{2\alpha}{2\alpha+1}}\sqrt{E_{2}}+\hat{{\mathfrak{r}}}\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}+\frac{1+tB_{\tilde{f}}}{N}\bigg\}.

∎

Proof of Corollary
15
.

The bound in Corollary
15
can be directly obtained if we choose the parameter
r
of tubal ranks in
\mathfrak{F}_{\textnormal{{r}}}
by
r_{l}=\min\{\lceil\big(LV_{0}B_{\tilde{f}}B_{l}^{-1}\big)^{1/\alpha}\rceil,d_{l},d_{l-1}\}
.
∎

Appendix F
Useful Notions and Lemmas

In this section, we provide several notions and lemmas which are used in the previous analysis.

F.1
Tools for Analyzing General DNNs

We briefly list the tools used in this paper for analyzing the generalization error of general DNNs, including Rademacher complexity, covering number, and concentration inequalities,
etc.
.

Definition 13

(Rademacher complexity)
.

Given an i.i.d. sample
S:=\{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}
of size
N
and a function class
{\cal H}
, the empirical Rademacher complexity of
{\cal H}
is defined as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{R}_{S}({\cal H}):=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,\varepsilon_{N}}\left[\sup_{h\in{\cal H}}\frac{1}{N}\varepsilon_{i}h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\right],

where
\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,\varepsilon_{N}
are i.i.d. Rademacher variables, i.e.,
\varepsilon_{i}
equals to
1
or
-1
with equal probability. The average Rademacher complexity is further defined as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \bar{R}_{N}=\mathbb{E}_{S}\hat{R}_{S}({\cal H}).

Lemma 29

(
[
4
]
)
.

Given an i.i.d. sample
S:=\{(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}
of
size
N
, a loss function
\ell(h(\cdot),y)
taking
values in
[0,B]
, the generalization error of any function
f
in hypothesis set
\mathcal{F}
satisfies

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathcal{L}(f)\leq\hat{\mathcal{L}}(f)+2\hat{R}_{S}(\ell\circ\mathcal{F})+3B\sqrt{\frac{t}{2N}},

(67)

with probability at least
1-e^{-t}
for all
t\geq 0
.

Lemma 30

(Talagrand’s contraction lemma
[
44
]
)
.

Given function set
\mathcal{F}
and
L_{\ell}
-Lipschtz function
\ell
, for a function sets defined as

l_{\mathcal{F}}:=\{\ell\circ f\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ f\in\mathcal{F}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{R}_{S}(\mathcal{F})\leq L_{\ell}\hat{R}_{S}(\mathcal{F}).

Definition 14

(
\epsilon
-covering net)
.

Let
\epsilon>0
and
(\mathcal{X},d(\cdot,\cdot))
be a metric space, where
d(\cdot,\cdot)
is a (pseudo)-metric.
We say
\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{X}
is an
\epsilon
-covering net of
\mathcal{X}
, if for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
, there exists
z\in\mathcal{Z}
such that
d(x,z)\leq\epsilon
. Define the smallest

|\mathcal{Z}|
as the
\epsilon
-covering number of
\mathcal{X}
and denote as
{\sf N}(\mathcal{X},d(\cdot,\cdot),\epsilon)
.

Given a traning dataset
S=\{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
and a function set
\mathcal{F}
. Consider the output space of
the space of
\mathcal{F}
restricted on
S
, i.e.,
\mathcal{F}|_{S}=\{\big(f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{1},y_{1}),\cdots,f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{N},y_{N})\big)^{\top}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ f\in\mathcal{F}\}
. Then, define a pseudo-norm of
\mathcal{F}|_{S}
as:
\left\|{f}\right\|_{S}:=N^{-1}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{i},y_{i})^{2}}
.
Then, the Rademacher complexity of
\mathcal{F}
could be upper bounded by the
\epsilon
-covering number of
\mathcal{F}
under
the empirical
l_{2}
-pseudo-metric by the Dudley’s inequality as follows:

Lemma 31

(Dudley’s integral inequality
[
47
]
)
.

The Rademacher complexity
\hat{R}_{S}(\mathcal{F})
satisfies

\displaystyle\penalty\ \hat{R}_{S}(\mathcal{F})\leq\inf_{\delta>0}\left[8\delta+\frac{12}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{\delta}^{\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{S}}\sqrt{\log{\sf N}(\mathcal{F},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{S},\epsilon)}d\epsilon\right].

(68)

Lemma 32

(Covering number of norm balls
[
47
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{\mathsf{B}}
be a
l_{p}
-norm ball with radius
W
. Let
d(\textnormal{{x}}_{1},\textnormal{{x}}_{2})=\left\|{\textnormal{{x}}_{1}-\textnormal{{x}}_{2}}\right\|_{p}
. Define the
\epsilon
-covering number of
\mathbf{\mathsf{B}}
as
{\sf N}(\mathbf{\mathsf{B}},d(\cdot,\cdot),\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\sf N}(\mathbf{\mathsf{B}},d(\cdot,\cdot),\epsilon)\leq\left(1+\frac{2W}{\epsilon}\right)^{d}.

(69)

Lemma 33

(Covering number of low-tubal-rank tensors)
.

For the set of tensors
\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}:=\{{\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n\times{\sf c}}\penalty\ \big|\penalty\ r_{\textnormal{t}}({\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}})\leq r,\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq 1\}
with
r\leq\min\{m,n\}
, its
\epsilon
-covering number can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\sf N}(\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}},\epsilon)\leq\left(\frac{9}{\epsilon}\right)^{(m+n+1)r{\sf c}}.

(70)

Proof.

Consider the reduced t-SVD
[
20
]
of a tensor
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}={\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
, where
*_{M}
denotes the t-product induced by the linear transform
M(\cdot)
defined in Eq. (
1
) ,
{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times r\times{\sf c}}
and
{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r\times{\sf c}}
are (semi)-t-orthogonal tensors, and
{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r\times{\sf c}}
is an f-diagonal tensor. As
{\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
, we have
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq 1
. The idea is to cover
\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
by covering the set of factor tensors
{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}},{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}
and
{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}
.

Let
\mathbf{\mathsf{D}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r\times r\times{\sf c}}
be the set of f-diagonal tensors with F-norm equal to one. We take
\mathbf{\mathsf{D}}^{c}
to be an
\epsilon/3
-covering net for
\mathbf{\mathsf{D}}
. Then by Lemma
32
, we have
|\mathbf{\mathsf{D}}^{c}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{r{\sf c}}
. Next, let
\mathbf{\mathsf{O}}_{m,r}=\{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times r\times{\sf c}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{\top}={\underline{\textnormal{{I}}}}\}
. To cover
\mathbf{\mathsf{O}}_{m,r}
, we consider the
\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{\infty,2,2}
-norm defined as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}}\right\|_{\infty,2,2}:=\max_{i}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}_{:,i,:}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}.

Let
\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}:=\{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times r\times{\sf c}}\penalty\ |\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}}\right\|_{\infty,2,2}\leq 1\}
. Then, we have
\mathbf{\mathsf{O}}_{m,r}\subset\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}
by the definition of t-orthogonal tensors. Letting
\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}^{c}
be an
\epsilon/3
-covering net of
\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}
, then we obtain
|\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}^{c}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{mr{\sf c}}
by Theorem
32
. Similarly, an
\epsilon/3
-covering net of
\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{n,r}
satisfies
|\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{n,r}^{c}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{nr{\sf c}}
.

Now construct a set
\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}^{c}=\{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}\penalty\ |\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}^{c},\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{D}}^{c},{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{n,r}^{c}\}
. Then, we have

|\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}^{c}|=|\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}^{c}|\cdot|{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}|\cdot|\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{n,r}^{c}|\leq(9/\epsilon)^{(m+n+1)r{\sf c}}.

Net, we will show that
\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}^{c}
is an
\epsilon
-covering net of
\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
, i.e., for any
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
, there is an
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}^{c}
satisfying
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\epsilon
.

Given
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
, consider its reduced t-SVD as
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}={\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
. Then, there exists
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}^{c}={\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}
with
{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{m,r}^{c},\penalty\ {\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{D}}^{c},{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{Q}}_{n,r}^{c}
satisying

\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\infty,2,2}\leq\epsilon/3
,
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{F}\leq\epsilon/3
, and
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\infty,2,2}\leq\epsilon/3
. This gives

\displaystyle\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}+{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}

\displaystyle+{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}\bigg\|_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}+\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle+\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}+\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle+\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}+\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c})*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}+\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}.

For the first term, note that since
{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}
is a t-orthogonal tensor,

\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}.

and

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})_{:,i,:}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}_{i,i,:}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})_{:,i,:}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}_{i,i,:}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}_{i,i,:}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2})\max_{i}\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})_{:,i,:}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}^{2}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\infty,2,2}

\displaystyle\leq({\epsilon}/{3})^{2}.

Hence,
\left\|{({\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c})*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\epsilon/3
. Similarly, we have

\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c})^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\epsilon/3
.
The middle term can be bounded
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}*_{M}({\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c})*_{M}{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{\top}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{S}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\epsilon/3
due to the property of t-orthognal tensors
{\underline{\textnormal{{U}}}}^{c}
and
{\underline{\textnormal{{V}}}}^{c}

[
20
]
. Therefore, for any
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
, there is an
{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}^{c}\in\mathbf{\mathsf{T}}_{r}^{c}
satisfying
\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}-{\underline{\textnormal{{X}}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\epsilon
.
∎

Lemma 34

(Covering number bounds for composition and addtion
[
10
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
be classes of functions on normed space
(\mathcal{X},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Y},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{Y}})
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of
c
-Lipschitz functions
(\mathcal{Y},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{Y}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Z},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{Z}})
. Then for any
X\in\mathcal{X}^{N}
and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{1}},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{2}},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}}>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\sf N}(\{f_{1}+f_{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}\},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{1}}+\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{2}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X})\leq{\sf N}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{1}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X}){\sf N}(\mathcal{F}_{2},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{2}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X}).

and

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\sf N}(\{f\circ f_{1}\penalty\ |\penalty\ f\in\mathcal{F},f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}\},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}}+c\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{1}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X})\leq{\sf N}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{1}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X})\sup_{f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}}{\sf N}(\mathcal{F},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{f_{1}(X)}).

Specifically, if
\mathcal{F}=\{f\}
is a singleton, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ {\sf N}(\{f\circ f_{1}\penalty\ |\penalty\ f\in\mathcal{F},f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}\},c\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{1}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X})\leq{\sf N}(\mathcal{F}_{1},\epsilon_{\mathcal{F}_{1}},\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X}).

Lemma 35

(Simplifided Talagrand’s concentration inequality
[
42
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a function class on
\mathcal{X}
that is separable with respect to
L_{\infty}
-norm, and
\{\textnormal{{x}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
be i.i.d. random variables in
\mathcal{X}
. Furthermore, suppose there exist constants
V\geq
and
U\geq 0
such that
V=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}[(f-\mathbb{E}[f])^{2}]
and
U=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|{f}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}
. Letting
Z:=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}f(\textnormal{{x}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}[f]|
,
then it holds for all
t>0
that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{P}\left[Z\geq 2\mathbb{E}[Z]+\sqrt{\frac{2Vt}{N}}+\frac{2Ut}{N}\right]\leq e^{-t}.

F.2
Some Results for Analyzing t-NNs

In this subsection, we present several fundamental
statements described as lemmas for analyzing t-NNs.
First, we give Lemmas
36
-
38
, which are used in the analysis of the t-product layers.

Lemma 36
.

Let
\sigma(\cdot):\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a
L_{\sigma}
-Lipschitz function, i.e.,
|\sigma(x)-\sigma(y)|\leq L_{\sigma}|x-y|,\forall x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. If it is applied element-wisely to any two real vectors
x
and
y
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\sigma(\textnormal{{x}})-\sigma(\textnormal{{y}})}\right\|_{l_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq L_{\sigma}\left\|{\textnormal{{x}}-\textnormal{{y}}}\right\|_{l_{p}}.

Lemma 37
.

The following inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}},\quad\textnormal{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}.

Proof.

According to the definition of the
M
transform in Eq. (
1
) and the orthogonality of
M
, we have

\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}=\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{T}}}_{M}}\right\|\leq\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{T}}}_{M}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{M({\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{T}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}},

and

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}\cdot\widetilde{\textnormal{{x}}}_{M}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\overset{(i)}{\leq}\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{W}}}_{M}}\right\|\left\|{\widetilde{\textnormal{{x}}}_{M}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}=\left\|{\textnormal{{W}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{sp}}\left\|{\textnormal{{x}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\left\|{\textnormal{{W}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\textnormal{{x}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}},

where inequality
(i)
holds because
\left\|{\textnormal{{A}}\textnormal{{B}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq\left\|{\textnormal{{A}}}\right\|\left\|{\textnormal{{B}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}
for any matrices
\textnormal{{A}},\textnormal{{B}}
with appropriate dimensions.
∎

Lemma 38

(The t-product layer is Lipschitz continuous)
.

Suppose the activation function is
L_{\sigma}
-Lipschitz, then a
layer of t-product layer

h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})=\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}})
is at most
L_{\sigma}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}
-Lipschitz.

Proof.

According to the Lipschitzness of the activation function, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{1})-h(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{2})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{1})-\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{2})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq L_{\sigma}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{1}-{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{2}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=L_{\sigma}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}*_{M}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{1}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{2})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=L_{\sigma}\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{1}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}_{2}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}.

∎

We then present Lemma
39
and Lemma
40
which are
used in upper bounding the input and output of t-NNs in adversarial settings.

Lemma 39
.

Given a fixed example
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times 1\times{\sf c}}
, if an adversary
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}
satisfies
R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|{\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\leq B_{x}+\xi{\sf C}_{R_{\textnormal{a}}}.

Lemma 40
.

The
L_{\infty}
-norm of any
f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}};{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}})\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
defined on the set of input examples
\mathcal{X}
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\left\|{\tilde{f}}\right\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq B_{\tilde{f}}:=B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}.

(71)

The diameter of
\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}
is can be upper bounded as follows

\displaystyle\penalty\ {D}_{\tilde{f}}:=2\sup_{\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\left\|{\tilde{f}}\right\|_{S}\leq 2B_{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}.

(72)

Proof.

For any
f\in\mathfrak{F}
, given an example
(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)\in\mathcal{X}\times\{\pm 1\}
, let
\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*}\in\Arginf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}yf(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})
be one adversarial example. Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ |\tilde{f}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}},y)|

\displaystyle=|\inf_{R_{\textnormal{a}}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}-\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})\leq\xi}y_{i}f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=|f(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*})|

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=|\textnormal{{w}}^{\top}\textnormal{\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\footnotesize\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{vec}}}}}}}\big(\textnormal{{h}}^{(L)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*})\big)|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}}\right\|\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}*_{M}\textnormal{{h}}^{(L-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*}_{i}))}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}}\right\|\left\|{\sigma({\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}*_{M}\textnormal{{h}}^{(L-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*}))-\sigma(\textnormal{{0}})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}}\right\|\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}*_{M}\textnormal{{h}}^{(L-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\textnormal{{w}}}\right\|\left\|{{\underline{\textnormal{{W}}}}^{(L)}}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}\left\|{\textnormal{{h}}^{(L-1)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\cdots

\displaystyle\leq{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}\left\|{\textnormal{{h}}^{(0)}(\underline{\textnormal{{x}}}^{*})}\right\|_{\textnormal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq B_{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}

\displaystyle=:B_{\tilde{f}},

which also implies

\displaystyle\penalty\ {D}_{\tilde{f}}=2\max_{\tilde{f}\in\mathfrak{F}^{\textnormal{adv}}}\left\|{\tilde{f}}\right\|_{S}\leq 2B_{\textnormal{{w}}}\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}B_{x,R_{\textnormal{a}},\xi}.

∎

Lemma
41
helps upper bounding the F-norm of residuals after low-tubal-rank approximation of the weight tensors of t-NNs under Assumption
13
.

Lemma 41
.

Given constants
a>0,\alpha>1
, suppose a sequence
\{z_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
satisfying polynomial decay
z_{j}\leq aj^{-\alpha}
, then for any positive integer
n
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j>n}z_{j}\leq\frac{an^{1-\alpha}}{\alpha-1}.

Proof.

We compute the sum of the sequence using integration as follows:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j>n}z_{j}\leq\int_{n}^{\infty}at^{-\alpha}{\sf d}t=\frac{a}{\alpha-1}t^{1-\alpha}\big|_{n}^{\infty}\leq\frac{an^{1-\alpha}}{\alpha-1}.

∎
